      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-additive-elimination",
        "exact_quote": "With the rows, good-tuple law, and modulus of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:row-directions\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:row-directions\">17</a>, fix $J_0\\in\\mathbb N$ and put $$L(\\boldsymbol p)=\\left\\lfloor\\frac{R}{J_0M(\\boldsymbol p)}\\right\\rfloor.\n \\label{eq:correlation-shift-length}$$ Independently conditional on the primes, let every $u_R^0,u_R^1$ be uniform on $[0,L(\\boldsymbol p))\\cap\\mathbb Z$, for $R\\ne *$. Then $$\\left|\\mathbb E_{\\boldsymbol p,z}\\prod_{I\\in\\mathcal R}f_I(\\ell_I(z))\n \\right|^{2^d}\n \\le C_m\\left|\\mathbb E_{\\boldsymbol p,z,u}\n       \\prod_{\\omega\\in\\{0,1\\}^d}\n       g_*\\left(\\ell_*(z)+M(\\boldsymbol p)\n                    \\sum_{R\\ne *}u_R^{\\omega_R}\\right)\\right|+o(1).\n \\label{eq:additive-elimination-output}$$ The constants are independent of $J_0$; the error is for fixed $J_0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-164.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-additive-elimination",

Then $$\\left|\\mathbb E_{\\boldsymbol p,z}\\prod_{I\\in\\mathcal R}f_I(\\ell_I(z))\n \\right|^{2^d}\n \\le C_m\\left|\\mathbb E_{\\boldsymbol p,z,u}\n       \\prod_{\\omega\\in\\{0,1\\}^d}\n       g_*\\left(\\ell_*(z)+M(\\boldsymbol p)\n                    \\sum_{R\\ne *}u_R^{\\omega_R}\\right)\\right|+o(1).\n \\label{eq:additive-elimination-output}$$ The constants are independent of $J_0$; the error is for fixed $J_0$.",

      "exact_statement": "With the rows, good-tuple law, and modulus of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:row-directions\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:row-directions\">17</a>, fix $J_0\\in\\mathbb N$ and put $$L(\\boldsymbol p)=\\left\\lfloor\\frac{R}{J_0M(\\boldsymbol p)}\\right\\rfloor.\n \\label{eq:correlation-shift-length}$$ Independently conditional on the primes, let every $u_R^0,u_R^1$ be uniform on $[0,L(\\boldsymbol p))\\cap\\mathbb Z$, for $R\\ne *$. Then $$\\left|\\mathbb E_{\\boldsymbol p,z}\\prod_{I\\in\\mathcal R}f_I(\\ell_I(z))\n \\right|^{2^d}\n \\le C_m\\left|\\mathbb E_{\\boldsymbol p,z,u}\n       \\prod_{\\omega\\in\\{0,1\\}^d}\n       g_*\\left(\\ell_*(z)+M(\\boldsymbol p)\n                    \\sum_{R\\ne *}u_R^{\\omega_R}\\right)\\right|+o(1).\n \\label{eq:additive-elimination-output}$$ The constants are independent of $J_0$; the error is for fixed $J_0$.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-additive-elimination",
          "exact_quote": "With the rows, good-tuple law, and modulus of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:row-directions\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:row-directions\">17</a>, fix $J_0\\in\\mathbb N$ and put $$L(\\boldsymbol p)=\\left\\lfloor\\frac{R}{J_0M(\\boldsymbol p)}\\right\\rfloor.\n \\label{eq:correlation-shift-length}$$ Independently conditional on the primes, let every $u_R^0,u_R^1$ be uniform on $[0,L(\\boldsymbol p))\\cap\\mathbb Z$, for $R\\ne *$.",

        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-correlation-test",
        "exact_quote": "For every $m\\ge2$ and every nonsingleton target support $J_*$, the construction above determines a finite family of integer polynomial templates $D$, with good prime-tuple laws, such that the following holds for every fixed master chain and valid gap $l$. There are an integer $1\\le d\\le K_m-1$, a constant $C_m$, and $\\theta>0$, all bounded in terms of $m$ alone, for which $$\\boxed{\\quad\n |\\mathcal C|\n \\le o(1)+C_m\\left|\n  \\mathbb E_{\\boldsymbol p,y,u}\n  \\prod_{\\omega\\subseteq[d]}\n  g_*\\left(y+M(\\boldsymbol p)\n                  \\sum_{R\\in\\omega}(u_R^1-u_R^0)\\right)\n                       \\right|^{\\theta}.\n \\quad}\n \\label{eq:correlation-test}$$ Here $y$ has law $\\mu_{i_{a_*}}$, independently of the primes and shifts; the primes have the normalized good-tuple law of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:row-directions\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:row-directions\">17</a>; $M(\\boldsymbol p)$ is given by <a href=\"#eq:correlation-modulus\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:correlation-modulus\">[eq:correlation-modulus]</a>; and the shifts have the conditional laws in <a href=\"#eq:correlation-shift-length\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:correlation-shift-length\">[eq:correlation-shift-length]</a>. One may take $\\theta=2^{-(q_{\\mathrm{mask}}+d)}$. The gap length $R_l$ is divisible by every $M(\\boldsymbol p)$ that occurs.*\n\n*The estimate is uniform over all the masks and functions in <a href=\"#eq:correlation-initial\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:correlation-initial\">[eq:correlation-initial]</a>, including $g_*$, and over each fixed finite set of positive integers $J_0$. Neither $d$, $C_m$, nor $\\theta^{-1}$ depends on $N$, on the scales, or on the functions. The polynomial templates and direction choices are fixed before $M$ and the prime pools are chosen. In particular, the same tests can be used both for a bounded error and for an error bounded by $1+\\nu_{a_*}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-164.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rem-correlation-dual-interface",
        "exact_quote": "The base vertex in <a href=\"#eq:correlation-test\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:correlation-test\">[eq:correlation-test]</a> is $g_*(y)$. All its other vertices may therefore be regarded as inputs in a dual test $$\\mathcal D(y)=\\mathbb E_{\\boldsymbol p,u}e(\\boldsymbol p)\n  \\prod_{\\emptyset\\ne\\omega\\subseteq[d]}\n  h_{\\omega,\\boldsymbol p}\\left(y+M(\\boldsymbol p)\n              \\sum_{R\\in\\omega}(u_R^1-u_R^0)\\right),\n \\quad |e|\\le1,\\quad |h_{\\omega,\\boldsymbol p}|\\le1+\\nu_{a_*}.$$ In particular, choosing every $h_{\\omega,\\boldsymbol p}=g_*$ and $e=1$ recovers its cube expectation as $\\mathbb E_{\\mu_{i_{a_*}}}g_*\\mathcal D$. The enlarged test class, including independently chosen inputs and bounded prime-dependent signs, is fixed without reference to a dense model. Proposition <a href=\"#prop:correlation-test\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:correlation-test\">19</a> itself asserts the inequality for the repeated target function; the pseudorandomness of the enlarged test class is proved in Section <a href=\"#sec:prediction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:prediction\">5</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-164.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

The enlarged test class, including independently chosen inputs and bounded prime-dependent signs, is fixed without reference to a dense model.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-dual-pseudorandomness",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $b\\geq0$ and dual tests $\\mathcal D_1,\\ldots,\\mathcal D_b$ at the same block, $$\\label{eq:prediction-dual-products}\n \\mathbb E_{\\mu_i}(\\nu-1)\\prod_{q=1}^b\\mathcal D_q=o(1)$$ uniformly over their inputs. The tests may use different permissible gaps and different cube types. If $d_*$ bounds the dimensions of these types and $A_*=2^{2^{d_*}-1}$, then for every fixed positive integer $b$, $$\\label{eq:prediction-dual-moments}\n \\mathbb E_{\\mu_i}(1+\\nu)|\\mathcal D|^b\\leq2A_*^b+o(1).$$ For any fixed $K>A_*$, replacing every test by its clipping to $[-K,K]$ changes it by $o(1)$ in every fixed $L^p((1+\\nu)\\mu_i)$ norm, and <a href=\"#eq:prediction-dual-products\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:prediction-dual-products\">[eq:prediction-dual-products]</a> remains true for the clipped tests.",
        "source_id": "PM-164.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-dual-pseudorandomness",
          "exact_quote": "For every fixed $b\\geq0$ and dual tests $\\mathcal D_1,\\ldots,\\mathcal D_b$ at the same block, $$\\label{eq:prediction-dual-products}\n \\mathbb E_{\\mu_i}(\\nu-1)\\prod_{q=1}^b\\mathcal D_q=o(1)$$ uniformly over their inputs.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-energy-selection",
        "exact_quote": "For every $\\varepsilon>0$, a master count $N$ depending only on $n,r,|\\mathcal A|,\\varepsilon$ can be chosen so that there are indices $$k_1<j_1<k_2<j_2<\\cdots<k_n<j_n$$ and $[0,1]$-valued piecewise models $S_{B,a,c}$ at pivot $i=j_u$ on intervals of length $R_{k_u}$ such that $$\\label{eq:prediction-coarse-approximation}\n \\|S_{B,a,c}-P_{j_u,k_u}F_{B,a,c}\\|_2\\leq\\varepsilon.$$ For a chain on the principal indices $j_1,\\ldots,j_n$, let $l$ be the padding index immediately before its first pivot. At every block $B=T\\cup\\{j_u\\}$ of the chain, $$\\label{eq:prediction-fine-projection}\n \\|P_{j_u,l}(F_{B,a,c}-S_{B,a,c})\\|_2\\leq2\\varepsilon.$$ The families $S$ have bounded complexity after $N$ and $\\varepsilon$ are fixed.",
        "source_id": "PM-164.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-energy-selection",
          "exact_quote": "For every $\\varepsilon>0$, a master count $N$ depending only on $n,r,|\\mathcal A|,\\varepsilon$ can be chosen so that there are indices $$k_1<j_1<k_2<j_2<\\cdots<k_n<j_n$$ and $[0,1]$-valued piecewise models $S_{B,a,c}$ at pivot $i=j_u$ on intervals of length $R_{k_u}$ such that $$\\label{eq:prediction-coarse-approximation}\n \\|S_{B,a,c}-P_{j_u,k_u}F_{B,a,c}\\|_2\\leq\\varepsilon.$$ For a chain on the principal indices $j_1,\\ldots,j_n$, let $l$ be the padding index immediately before its first pivot.         {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-energy-selection",
          "exact_quote": "For every $\\varepsilon>0$, a master count $N$ depending only on $n,r,|\\mathcal A|,\\varepsilon$ can be chosen so that there are indices $$k_1<j_1<k_2<j_2<\\cdots<k_n<j_n$$ and $[0,1]$-valued piecewise models $S_{B,a,c}$ at pivot $i=j_u$ on intervals of length $R_{k_u}$ such that $$\\label{eq:prediction-coarse-approximation}\n \\|S_{B,a,c}-P_{j_u,k_u}F_{B,a,c}\\|_2\\leq\\varepsilon.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-164.1"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-subgroup-inverse",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a cube type of dimension $d$, a gap $l<i$, and $J_0\\geq1$. Assume $2(2^d)-2\\leq s$. For every $\\gamma>0$ there is $\\kappa=\\kappa(d,J_0,\\gamma)>0$, independent of the master count, the gap, the cutoffs, and the cell probabilities, with the following property. If $h:\\mathbb Z\\to[-1,1]$ is a family with $\\|P_{i,l}h\\|_2<\\kappa$, then $$\\label{eq:prediction-subgroup-conclusion}\n \\lim_{\\mathcal U}\\left|\n \\mathbb E_{\\boldsymbol p,y,u}\n \\prod_{\\omega\\subset[d]}\n h\\left(y+M_{\\boldsymbol p}\n       \\sum_{j\\in\\omega}(u_j^1-u_j^0)\\right)\\right|\n \\leq\\gamma+O_d(J_0^{-1}).$$ Here $y\\sim\\mu_i$, and primes and shifts have the laws in <a href=\"#eq:prediction-dual-test\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:prediction-dual-test\">[eq:prediction-dual-test]</a>. The constant in the last term depends only on $d$.",
        "source_id": "PM-164.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-quantitative-leibman",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a rational filtered nilmanifold as above, a number $d$ of variables, and $0<\\delta<1$. There is a constant $A$, depending only on these data, with the following property. If $P:\\mathbb Z^d\\to G$ is polynomial for this filtration and $(P(v)\\Gamma)_{v\\in\\{0,\\ldots,N-1\\}^d}$ is not $\\delta$-equidistributed, then there is a nonzero horizontal character $\\xi$ of size at most $A$ such that, on writing $$\\xi(P(v))=\\sum_I\\alpha_I\\binom vI,\n \\qquad \\binom vI=\\prod_{j=1}^d\\binom{v_j}{I_j},$$ one has $$N^{\\abs I}\\norm{\\alpha_I}_{\\mathbb R/\\mathbb Z}\\leq A\n \\qquad (\\abs I>0).\n \\label{eq:rough-leibman-binomial}$$ Here $\\delta$-equidistribution means comparison with Haar probability to error at most $\\delta$ against every observable of Lipschitz norm at most one. The constant $A$ is independent of the coefficients of $P$ and of $N$.*\n\n\n\n*The following consequence will be used. Fix also $c,C,B,\\eta>0$. Let $\\mathcal Q\\subseteq[-CZ,CZ]^d$ be an axis-parallel box with each side at least $cZ$, sampled at its integer points. If $$\\abs{\\mathbb E_{x\\in\\mathcal Q\\cap\\mathbb Z^d}F(P(x)\\Gamma)\n       -\\int_{G/\\Gamma}F}\\geq\\eta,\n \\qquad \\norm F_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lip}}}\\leq B,\n \\label{eq:rough-haar-obstruction}$$ then, for sufficiently large $Z$, there is a nonzero horizontal character in a fixed finite list such that, writing $\\xi(P(x))=\\sum_I\\theta_Ix^I$ in ordinary monomials, $$\\norm{\\theta_I}_{\\mathbb R/\\mathbb Z}\\leq A'Z^{-\\abs I}\n \\qquad (\\abs I>0).\n \\label{eq:rough-leibman-monomial}$$ The list and $A'$ depend only on the fixed filtered nilmanifold, $d,c,C,B,\\eta$. The same assertions hold with the nilmanifold and its filtration chosen from a fixed finite list.",
        "source_id": "PM-164.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rough-interpolation",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $e\\geq1$, $q\\geq e$, and $A>0$. For a fixed integer $H\\geq2q+2$, let $$t_z=t_0+mz\\in[S,2S),\\qquad 0\\leq z<H,$$ where $m$ is a positive integer with all prime factors at most $w$, and $\\gcd(t_z,W)=1$ for every $z$, with $W=\\prod_{p\\leq w}p$. Suppose $w,S\\to\\infty$ and $Z/S^a\\to\\infty$ for every fixed $a>0$. Let $\\theta(z)$ be a real polynomial such that $\\deg(t_z^e\\theta(z))\\leq q$ and $$\\norm{t_z^e\\theta(z)}_{\\mathbb R/\\mathbb Z}\\leq A(S/Z)^e\n \\qquad(0\\leq z<H).\n \\label{eq:rough-interpolation-input}$$ For all sufficiently large $w$ there is a rational polynomial $M(z)$, of degree at most $q-e$, integral at every index $0\\leq z<H$, such that $$\\abs{\\theta(z)-M(z)}\\leq C_{q}A Z^{-e}\n \\qquad(0\\leq z<H).\n \\label{eq:rough-interpolation-output}$$ Its coefficient denominators have only prime factors at most $w$. The constant in <a href=\"#eq:rough-interpolation-output\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:rough-interpolation-output\">[eq:rough-interpolation-output]</a> is independent of $H$. The threshold for $w$ is permitted to depend on $H$.",
        "source_id": "PM-164.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-rough-interpolation",

Suppose $w,S\\to\\infty$ and $Z/S^a\\to\\infty$ for every fixed $a>0$. The constant in <a href=\"#eq:rough-interpolation-output\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:rough-interpolation-output\">[eq:rough-interpolation-output]</a> is independent of $H$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-rough-step",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $G/\\Gamma$, a number $d\\geq1$ of spatial variables, a degree bound $D$, and constants $c,C,B>0$. Along an arbitrary sequence $w\\to\\infty$, let $L,S,Z$ satisfy $$W\\mid L,\\quad L\\text{ is }w\\text{-smooth},\\quad\n S\\to\\infty,\\quad S/L\\to\\infty,\\quad\n Z/S^a\\to\\infty\\quad\\text{for every fixed }a>0.\n \\label{eq:rough-scales}$$ Fix a residue $r$ modulo $L$ with $\\gcd(r,W)=1$, and put $\\mathcal T=[S,2S)\\cap(r+L\\mathbb Z)$. Let $P_t(x)$ be $G$-valued, with logarithmic coordinates polynomial jointly in $(t,x)\\in\\mathbb R\\times\\mathbb R^d$ of total degree at most $D$. These polynomials and their coefficients may change with $w$.*\n\n\n*For every $\\eta>0$, the proportion of $t\\in\\mathcal T$ for which there exist a box $\\mathcal Q\\subseteq[-CZ,CZ]^d$ with all sides at least $cZ$, a vector $b\\in\\mathbb Z^d$, and an observable $F:G/\\Gamma\\to\\mathbb C$ with $\\norm F_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lip}}}\\leq B$, such that $$\\abs{\\mathbb E_{x\\in\\mathcal Q\\cap\\mathbb Z^d}F(P_t(x)\\Gamma)\n       -\\mathbb E_{x\\in\\mathcal Q\\cap(b+t\\mathbb Z^d)}F(P_t(x)\\Gamma)}\n       \\geq\\eta\n \\label{eq:rough-discrepancy}$$ tends to zero.*\n\n*This conclusion holds for every sequence of the allowed data. In particular the exceptional proportion is uniform over the polynomial coefficients, the residue $r$, and all the boxes, residue vectors and observables in <a href=\"#eq:rough-discrepancy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:rough-discrepancy\">[eq:rough-discrepancy]</a>. The latter three may be chosen separately for every $t$ without any polynomial dependence. The same conclusion holds for a fixed finite list of nilmanifolds and fixed complexity bounds.",
        "source_id": "PM-164.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-rough-step",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $G/\\Gamma$, a number $d\\geq1$ of spatial variables, a degree bound $D$, and constants $c,C,B>0$. Along an arbitrary sequence $w\\to\\infty$, let $L,S,Z$ satisfy $$W\\mid L,\\quad L\\text{ is }w\\text{-smooth},\\quad\n S\\to\\infty,\\quad S/L\\to\\infty,\\quad\n Z/S^a\\to\\infty\\quad\\text{for every fixed }a>0.\n \\label{eq:rough-scales}$$ Fix a residue $r$ modulo $L$ with $\\gcd(r,W)=1$, and put $\\mathcal T=[S,2S)\\cap(r+L\\mathbb Z)$. Let $P_t(x)$ be $G$-valued, with logarithmic coordinates polynomial jointly in $(t,x)\\in\\mathbb R\\times\\mathbb R^d$ of total degree at most $D$. These polynomials and their coefficients may change with $w$.*\n\n\n*For every $\\eta>0$, the proportion of $t\\in\\mathcal T$ for which there exist a box $\\mathcal Q\\subseteq[-CZ,CZ]^d$ with all sides at least $cZ$, a vector $b\\in\\mathbb Z^d$, and an observable $F:G/\\Gamma\\to\\mathbb C$ with $\\norm F_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lip}}}\\leq B$, such that $$\\abs{\\mathbb E_{x\\in\\mathcal Q\\cap\\mathbb Z^d}F(P_t(x)\\Gamma)\n       -\\mathbb E_{x\\in\\mathcal Q\\cap(b+t\\mathbb Z^d)}F(P_t(x)\\Gamma)}\n       \\geq\\eta\n \\label{eq:rough-discrepancy}$$ tends to zero.*\n\n*This conclusion holds for every sequence of the allowed data. In particular the exceptional proportion is uniform over the polynomial coefficients, the residue $r$, and all the boxes, residue vectors and observables in <a href=\"#eq:rough-discrepancy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:rough-discrepancy\">[eq:rough-discrepancy]</a>. The latter three may be chosen separately for every $t$ without any polynomial dependence. The same conclusion holds for a fixed finite list of nilmanifolds and fixed complexity bounds.",
          "source_id": "PM-164.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Fix $G/\\Gamma$, a number $d\\geq1$ of spatial variables, a degree bound $D$, and constants $c,C,B>0$. Along an arbitrary sequence $w\\to\\infty$, let $L,S,Z$ satisfy $$W\\mid L,\\quad L\\text{ is }w\\text{-smooth},\\quad\n S\\to\\infty,\\quad S/L\\to\\infty,\\quad\n Z/S^a\\to\\infty\\quad\\text{for every fixed }a>0.\n \\label{eq:rough-scales}$$ Fix a residue $r$ modulo $L$ with $\\gcd(r,W)=1$, and put $\\mathcal T=[S,2S)\\cap(r+L\\mathbb Z)$. Let $P_t(x)$ be $G$-valued, with logarithmic coordinates polynomial jointly in $(t,x)\\in\\mathbb R\\times\\mathbb R^d$ of total degree at most $D$. These polynomials and their coefficients may change with $w$.*\n\n\n*For every $\\eta>0$, the proportion of $t\\in\\mathcal T$ for which there exist a box $\\mathcal Q\\subseteq[-CZ,CZ]^d$ with all sides at least $cZ$, a vector $b\\in\\mathbb Z^d$, and an observable $F:G/\\Gamma\\to\\mathbb C$ with $\\norm F_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lip}}}\\leq B$, such that $$\\abs{\\mathbb E_{x\\in\\mathcal Q\\cap\\mathbb Z^d}F(P_t(x)\\Gamma)\n       -\\mathbb E_{x\\in\\mathcal Q\\cap(b+t\\mathbb Z^d)}F(P_t(x)\\Gamma)}\n       \\geq\\eta\n \\label{eq:rough-discrepancy}$$ tends to zero.*\n\n*This conclusion holds for every sequence of the allowed data. In particular the exceptional proportion is uniform over the polynomial coefficients, the residue $r$, and all the boxes, residue vectors and observables in <a href=\"#eq:rough-discrepancy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:rough-discrepancy\">[eq:rough-discrepancy]</a>. The latter three may be chosen separately for every $t$ without any polynomial dependence. The same conclusion holds for a fixed finite list of nilmanifolds and fixed complexity bounds.",
      "explanation": "For nilmanifold polynomial orbits, sampling along a rough progression step agrees with unrestricted sampling except for a vanishing proportion of steps, uniformly over boxes, residues and observables.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-rough-residual-step",
        "exact_quote": "In Proposition <a href=\"#prop:rough-step\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:rough-step\">28</a>, let $q$ be another positive integer, fixed while $t$ varies but permitted to change with $w$. Let $a\\in\\{0,\\ldots,q-1\\}^d$ also be independent of $t$. Suppose the spatial boxes have common scale $Z_0$, with $Z_0/q$ dominating every fixed power of $S$. Suppose $\\log P_t(a+qy)$ is polynomial jointly in $(t,y)$ with fixed degree bound, and the normalized boxes lie in fixed multiples of $[-Z_0/q,Z_0/q]^d$ with comparable sides. Then, outside an exceptional proportion tending to zero, averaging $P_t$ over the class $a+q\\mathbb Z^d$ agrees to any prescribed accuracy with averaging over any compatible class modulo $qt$, with the same uniformities in boxes and tests. No coprimality between $q$ and $t$ is assumed.",
        "source_id": "PM-164.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-rough-residual-step",
          "exact_quote": "In Proposition <a href=\"#prop:rough-step\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:rough-step\">28</a>, let $q$ be another positive integer, fixed while $t$ varies but permitted to change with $w$. Let $a\\in\\{0,\\ldots,q-1\\}^d$ also be independent of $t$. Suppose the spatial boxes have common scale $Z_0$, with $Z_0/q$ dominating every fixed power of $S$. Suppose $\\log P_t(a+qy)$ is polynomial jointly in $(t,y)$ with fixed degree bound, and the normalized boxes lie in fixed multiples of $[-Z_0/q,Z_0/q]^d$ with comparable sides. Then, outside an exceptional proportion tending to zero, averaging $P_t$ over the class $a+q\\mathbb Z^d$ agrees to any prescribed accuracy with averaging over any compatible class modulo $qt$, with the same uniformities in boxes and tests. No coprimality between $q$ and $t$ is assumed.",
          "source_id": "PM-164.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "In Proposition <a href=\"#prop:rough-step\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:rough-step\">28</a>, let $q$ be another positive integer, fixed while $t$ varies but permitted to change with $w$. Let $a\\in\\{0,\\ldots,q-1\\}^d$ also be independent of $t$. Suppose the spatial boxes have common scale $Z_0$, with $Z_0/q$ dominating every fixed power of $S$. Suppose $\\log P_t(a+qy)$ is polynomial jointly in $(t,y)$ with fixed degree bound, and the normalized boxes lie in fixed multiples of $[-Z_0/q,Z_0/q]^d$ with comparable sides. Then, outside an exceptional proportion tending to zero, averaging $P_t$ over the class $a+q\\mathbb Z^d$ agrees to any prescribed accuracy with averaging over any compatible class modulo $qt$, with the same uniformities in boxes and tests. No coprimality between $q$ and $t$ is assumed.",
      "explanation": "One rough factor can be removed from a larger step while a fixed residue class is retained, without assuming coprimality.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-rough-residual-step",
          "exact_quote": "In Proposition <a href=\"#prop:rough-step\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:rough-step\">28</a>, let $q$ be another positive integer, fixed while $t$ varies but permitted to change with $w$.",
          "source_id": "PM-164.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-upper-face-coordinates",
        "exact_quote": "The group $C^q(H_\\bullet)$ is connected, simply connected, and rational. Order the subsets of $[q]$ by increasing cardinality, breaking ties in any fixed way. Every element of $C^q(H_\\bullet)$ has a unique expression $$\\prod_{D\\subseteq[q]}(g_D)_D,\\qquad g_D\\in H_{|D|},\n \\label{eq:ordered-face-product}$$ in this order; the map from the face coefficients to the array is a diffeomorphism. An array in this group which is the identity except possibly at the all-ones vertex has its remaining entry in $H_q$. The group is invariant under permutations and reflections of cube coordinates. If $\\Gamma$ is a lattice in $H$, its image in $(H/\\Gamma)^{\\{0,1\\}^q}$ is compact.",
        "source_id": "PM-164.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-upper-face-coordinates",

An array in this group which is the identity except possibly at the all-ones vertex has its remaining entry in $H_q$. The group is invariant under permutations and reflections of cube coordinates. If $\\Gamma$ is a lattice in $H$, its image in $(H/\\Gamma)^{\\{0,1\\}^q}$ is compact.",
          "source_id": "PM-164.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cube-corner",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a connected simply connected nilpotent Lie group of step at most $s$, and let $\\Gamma$ be a lattice in $G$. Equip $G$ with its lower central filtration, with $G_0=G_1=G$. In dimension $s+1$, any $2^{s+1}-1$ vertices of a cube in the image of $C^{s+1}(G_\\bullet)$ determine the last vertex uniquely and continuously on the set of admissible partial cubes. Linear orbit cubes $$\\bigl(g^{b_0+\\sum_{j=1}^{s+1}e_jb_j}x\\bigr)_e$$ belong to this compact cube set.*\n\n*Consequently, for every $\\varepsilon>0$ and every bounded Lipschitz observable $F$ on $G/\\Gamma$, its value at the missing vertex is uniformly within $\\varepsilon$ of a finite sum of products of bounded continuous functions of the other individual vertices. Those individual functions may be taken Lipschitz and bounded by one, with scalar coefficients outside the products. For a fixed finite list of nilmanifolds and fixed bounds on $\\norm{F}_\\infty$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lip}}(F)$, finitely many such approximation recipes suffice at each accuracy.",
        "source_id": "PM-164.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cube-corner",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a connected simply connected nilpotent Lie group of step at most $s$, and let $\\Gamma$ be a lattice in $G$. In dimension $s+1$, any $2^{s+1}-1$ vertices of a cube in the image of $C^{s+1}(G_\\bullet)$ determine the last vertex uniquely and continuously on the set of admissible partial cubes. Linear orbit cubes $$\\bigl(g^{b_0+\\sum_{j=1}^{s+1}e_jb_j}x\\bigr)_e$$ belong to this compact cube set.*\n\n*Consequently, for every $\\varepsilon>0$ and every bounded Lipschitz observable $F$ on $G/\\Gamma$, its value at the missing vertex is uniformly within $\\varepsilon$ of a finite sum of products of bounded continuous functions of the other individual vertices. Those individual functions may be taken Lipschitz and bounded by one, with scalar coefficients outside the products. For a fixed finite list of nilmanifolds and fixed bounds on $\\norm{F}_\\infty$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lip}}(F)$, finitely many such approximation recipes suffice at each accuracy.",
          "source_id": "PM-164.1"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-adapted-factorization",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a connected simply connected nilpotent group $K$, a lattice $\\Lambda$, and a rational filtration $K_\\bullet$ satisfying <a href=\"#eq:cube-filtration\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cube-filtration\">[eq:cube-filtration]</a>. Let $L\\to\\infty$ along any sequence, and let $P_L$ be arbitrary adapted polynomials. After passing to a subsequence there exist a fixed rational filtration $H_\\bullet$ with $H_i\\subseteq K_i$, a positive integer $d_0$, rational elements $\\sigma_0,\\ldots,\\sigma_{d_0-1}$ of $K$, and factorizations $$P_L(b)\\Lambda\n =h_L(b)Q_L(b)\\gamma_L(b)\\Lambda,\n \\qquad\n Q_L(b)=\\prod_{j=1}^s a_{j,L}^{\\binom bj},\\quad a_{j,L}\\in H_j,\n \\label{eq:adapted-factorization}$$ such that the following hold.*\n\n1.  *The maps $\\beta\\mapsto h_L(L\\beta)$ converge locally uniformly on $\\mathbb R$ to a smooth map $h:\\mathbb R\\to K$. They and their first derivatives are bounded on every fixed compact interval, uniformly in $L$.*\n\n2.  *For integer $b$, $\\gamma_L(b)\\Lambda=\\sigma_{b\\bmod d_0}\\Lambda$.*\n\n\n3.  *If $1\\leq j\\leq s$ and $\\xi:H_j\\to\\mathbb R$ is a nonzero rational homomorphism annihilating $H_{j+1}$ and every $[H_i,H_{j-i}]$, $1\\leq i<j$, then $$L^j\\norm{\\xi(a_{j,L})}_{\\mathbb R/\\mathbb Z}\\longrightarrow\\infty.\n     \\label{eq:level-irrationality}$$ Here rational homomorphisms include all nonzero rational multiples of one another. No boundedness of the Taylor coefficients $a_{j,L}$ is asserted or required.",
        "source_id": "PM-164.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-adapted-factorization",

Let $L\\to\\infty$ along any sequence, and let $P_L$ be arbitrary adapted polynomials. After passing to a subsequence there exist a fixed rational filtration $H_\\bullet$ with $H_i\\subseteq K_i$, a positive integer $d_0$, rational elements $\\sigma_0,\\ldots,\\sigma_{d_0-1}$ of $K$, and factorizations $$P_L(b)\\Lambda\n =h_L(b)Q_L(b)\\gamma_L(b)\\Lambda,\n \\qquad\n Q_L(b)=\\prod_{j=1}^s a_{j,L}^{\\binom bj},\\quad a_{j,L}\\in H_j,\n \\label{eq:adapted-factorization}$$ such that the following hold.*\n\n1.  *The maps $\\beta\\mapsto h_L(L\\beta)$ converge locally uniformly on $\\mathbb R$ to a smooth map $h:\\mathbb R\\to K$. They and their first derivatives are bounded on every fixed compact interval, uniformly in $L$.*\n\n2.  *For integer $b$, $\\gamma_L(b)\\Lambda=\\sigma_{b\\bmod d_0}\\Lambda$.*\n\n\n3.  *If $1\\leq j\\leq s$ and $\\xi:H_j\\to\\mathbb R$ is a nonzero rational homomorphism annihilating $H_{j+1}$ and every $[H_i,H_{j-i}]$, $1\\leq i<j$, then $$L^j\\norm{\\xi(a_{j,L})}_{\\mathbb R/\\mathbb Z}\\longrightarrow\\infty.\n     \\label{eq:level-irrationality}$$ Here rational homomorphisms include all nonzero rational multiples of one another. No boundedness of the Taylor coefficients $a_{j,L}$ is asserted or required.",
          "source_id": "PM-164.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Fix a connected simply connected nilpotent group $K$, a lattice $\\Lambda$, and a rational filtration $K_\\bullet$ satisfying <a href=\"#eq:cube-filtration\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cube-filtration\">[eq:cube-filtration]</a>. Let $L\\to\\infty$ along any sequence, and let $P_L$ be arbitrary adapted polynomials. After passing to a subsequence there exist a fixed rational filtration $H_\\bullet$ with $H_i\\subseteq K_i$, a positive integer $d_0$, rational elements $\\sigma_0,\\ldots,\\sigma_{d_0-1}$ of $K$, and factorizations $$P_L(b)\\Lambda\n =h_L(b)Q_L(b)\\gamma_L(b)\\Lambda,\n \\qquad\n Q_L(b)=\\prod_{j=1}^s a_{j,L}^{\\binom bj},\\quad a_{j,L}\\in H_j,\n \\label{eq:adapted-factorization}$$ such that the following hold.*\n\n1.  *The maps $\\beta\\mapsto h_L(L\\beta)$ converge locally uniformly on $\\mathbb R$ to a smooth map $h:\\mathbb R\\to K$. They and their first derivatives are bounded on every fixed compact interval, uniformly in $L$.*\n\n2.  *For integer $b$, $\\gamma_L(b)\\Lambda=\\sigma_{b\\bmod d_0}\\Lambda$.*\n\n\n3.  *If $1\\leq j\\leq s$ and $\\xi:H_j\\to\\mathbb R$ is a nonzero rational homomorphism annihilating $H_{j+1}$ and every $[H_i,H_{j-i}]$, $1\\leq i<j$, then $$L^j\\norm{\\xi(a_{j,L})}_{\\mathbb R/\\mathbb Z}\\longrightarrow\\infty.\n     \\label{eq:level-irrationality}$$ Here rational homomorphisms include all nonzero rational multiples of one another. No boundedness of the Taylor coefficients $a_{j,L}$ is asserted or required.",
      "explanation": "Adapted polynomial orbits factor, along a subsequence, into a smooth part, an equidistributed part in a fixed rational filtration with level irrationality, and a periodic part.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-local-cube-law",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:adapted-factorization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:adapted-factorization\">32</a>, retain the subsequence and its data. Define probability measures $$m_{\\beta,r}^{[q]}\n =\\text{Haar image on }\n h(\\beta)^{\\mathrm{diag}} C^q(H_\\bullet)\n \\sigma_r^{\\mathrm{diag}}\\Lambda^{\\{0,1\\}^q}/\n              \\Lambda^{\\{0,1\\}^q}.\n \\label{eq:local-cube-haar}$$ The empirical point laws satisfy $$\\frac1{\\lfloor L\\rfloor}\\sum_{0\\leq b<\\lfloor L\\rfloor}\n       \\delta_{P_L(b)\\Lambda}\n \\ \\Longrightarrow\n \\frac1{d_0}\\sum_{r=0}^{d_0-1}\\int_0^1 m_{\\beta,r}^{[0]}\\,d\\beta.\n \\label{eq:joint-point-law}$$ For every fixed cube dimension $q$, every $\\beta\\in(0,1)$, every residue $r\\bmod d_0$, and every bounded Lipschitz test $F$ on $(K/\\Lambda)^{\\{0,1\\}^q}$, $$\\lim_{\\alpha\\downarrow0}\\limsup_{L\\to\\infty}\n \\left|\n \\mathbb E_{\\substack{b_0/L\\in[\\beta,\\beta+\\alpha),\\ b_0\\equiv r\\ (d_0)\\\\\n                 b_j/L\\in[0,\\alpha),\\ b_j\\equiv0\\ (d_0),\\ 1\\leq j\\leq q}}\n F\\bigl((P_L(b_0+\\sum_{j=1}^q e_jb_j)\\Lambda)_e\\bigr)\n -\\int F\\,dm_{\\beta,r}^{[q]}\n \\right|=0.\n \\label{eq:local-cube-limit-order}$$ The inputs in this average are independent and uniform on the specified integer progressions. The same assertion holds for boxes with side lengths between $c\\alpha L$ and $C\\alpha L$, whose base coordinates are within $C\\alpha L$ of $\\beta L$ and whose increments have absolute value at most $C\\alpha L$, where $c,C>0$ are fixed before either limit. For fixed bounds the assertions are uniform over the tests and these boxes. In particular, arbitrary fixed-modulus progression restrictions are permitted if the base has residue $r$ and all increments have residue zero modulo $d_0$. Every progression modulus is fixed before the length limit, which always precedes the locality limit. The same factorization supplies these conclusions for every fixed $q$.",
        "source_id": "PM-164.1"
      },

The same assertion holds for boxes with side lengths between $c\\alpha L$ and $C\\alpha L$, whose base coordinates are within $C\\alpha L$ of $\\beta L$ and whose increments have absolute value at most $C\\alpha L$, where $c,C>0$ are fixed before either limit. For fixed bounds the assertions are uniform over the tests and these boxes.       "exact_statement": "Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:adapted-factorization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:adapted-factorization\">32</a>, retain the subsequence and its data. Define probability measures $$m_{\\beta,r}^{[q]}\n =\\text{Haar image on }\n h(\\beta)^{\\mathrm{diag}} C^q(H_\\bullet)\n \\sigma_r^{\\mathrm{diag}}\\Lambda^{\\{0,1\\}^q}/\n              \\Lambda^{\\{0,1\\}^q}.\n \\label{eq:local-cube-haar}$$ The empirical point laws satisfy $$\\frac1{\\lfloor L\\rfloor}\\sum_{0\\leq b<\\lfloor L\\rfloor}\n       \\delta_{P_L(b)\\Lambda}\n \\ \\Longrightarrow\n \\frac1{d_0}\\sum_{r=0}^{d_0-1}\\int_0^1 m_{\\beta,r}^{[0]}\\,d\\beta.\n \\label{eq:joint-point-law}$$ For every fixed cube dimension $q$, every $\\beta\\in(0,1)$, every residue $r\\bmod d_0$, and every bounded Lipschitz test $F$ on $(K/\\Lambda)^{\\{0,1\\}^q}$, $$\\lim_{\\alpha\\downarrow0}\\limsup_{L\\to\\infty}\n \\left|\n \\mathbb E_{\\substack{b_0/L\\in[\\beta,\\beta+\\alpha),\\ b_0\\equiv r\\ (d_0)\\\\\n                 b_j/L\\in[0,\\alpha),\\ b_j\\equiv0\\ (d_0),\\ 1\\leq j\\leq q}}\n F\\bigl((P_L(b_0+\\sum_{j=1}^q e_jb_j)\\Lambda)_e\\bigr)\n -\\int F\\,dm_{\\beta,r}^{[q]}\n \\right|=0.\n \\label{eq:local-cube-limit-order}$$ The inputs in this average are independent and uniform on the specified integer progressions.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-face-lift",
        "exact_quote": "Let $K=K'\\times K''$ be a connected simply connected nilpotent Lie group with lattice $\\Lambda=\\Lambda'\\times\\Lambda''$, and let $\\pi$ denote the first group projection and its induced map of nilmanifolds. Let $H\\subseteq K$ be a connected rational subgroup with a connected rational filtration $H_\\bullet$ satisfying <a href=\"#eq:cube-filtration\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cube-filtration\">[eq:cube-filtration]</a>. Let $h\\in K$, and let $\\sigma\\in K$ be rational. Let $S$ be a fixed automorphism of $K'$ preserving $\\Lambda'$. Suppose that, for every $1\\leq q\\leq s+1$, applying $S$ to the entries on any upper codimension-one face preserves setwise the projected cube coset $$\\pi\\left(h^{\\mathrm{diag}}C^q(H_\\bullet)\n                \\sigma^{\\mathrm{diag}}\\Lambda^{\\{0,1\\}^q}/\n                   \\Lambda^{\\{0,1\\}^q}\\right).\n \\label{eq:projected-face-coset}$$ Then there is a polynomial shear $T:\\mathfrak h\\to\\mathfrak h$ such that, for every $u\\in\\mathfrak h$, $$\\pi\\bigl(h\\exp(Tu)\\sigma\\Lambda\\bigr)\n =S\\bigl(\\pi(h\\exp(u)\\sigma\\Lambda)\\bigr).\n \\label{eq:joint-lift-identity}$$ The lift is a bijection of the cover. Its action on the other component is unrestricted. This conclusion applies separately to every point coset from Proposition <a href=\"#prop:local-cube-law\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:local-cube-law\">33</a>; no measurable choice of lifts as a function of $\\beta,r$ is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-164.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-face-lift",
          "exact_quote": "Let $K=K'\\times K''$ be a connected simply connected nilpotent Lie group with lattice $\\Lambda=\\Lambda'\\times\\Lambda''$, and let $\\pi$ denote the first group projection and its induced map of nilmanifolds. Let $H\\subseteq K$ be a connected rational subgroup with a connected rational filtration $H_\\bullet$ satisfying <a href=\"#eq:cube-filtration\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cube-filtration\">[eq:cube-filtration]</a>. Let $h\\in K$, and let $\\sigma\\in K$ be rational. Let $S$ be a fixed automorphism of $K'$ preserving $\\Lambda'$. Suppose that, for every $1\\leq q\\leq s+1$, applying $S$ to the entries on any upper codimension-one face preserves setwise the projected cube coset $$\\pi\\left(h^{\\mathrm{diag}}C^q(H_\\bullet)\n                \\sigma^{\\mathrm{diag}}\\Lambda^{\\{0,1\\}^q}/\n                   \\Lambda^{\\{0,1\\}^q}\\right).\n \\label{eq:projected-face-coset}$$ Then there is a polynomial shear $T:\\mathfrak h\\to\\mathfrak h$ such that, for every $u\\in\\mathfrak h$, $$\\pi\\bigl(h\\exp(Tu)\\sigma\\Lambda\\bigr)\n =S\\bigl(\\pi(h\\exp(u)\\sigma\\Lambda)\\bigr).\n \\label{eq:joint-lift-identity}$$ The lift is a bijection of the cover. Its action on the other component is unrestricted. This conclusion applies separately to every point coset from Proposition <a href=\"#prop:local-cube-law\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:local-cube-law\">33</a>; no measurable choice of lifts as a function of $\\beta,r$ is required.",
          "source_id": "PM-164.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $K=K'\\times K''$ be a connected simply connected nilpotent Lie group with lattice $\\Lambda=\\Lambda'\\times\\Lambda''$, and let $\\pi$ denote the first group projection and its induced map of nilmanifolds. Let $H\\subseteq K$ be a connected rational subgroup with a connected rational filtration $H_\\bullet$ satisfying <a href=\"#eq:cube-filtration\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cube-filtration\">[eq:cube-filtration]</a>. Let $h\\in K$, and let $\\sigma\\in K$ be rational. Let $S$ be a fixed automorphism of $K'$ preserving $\\Lambda'$. Suppose that, for every $1\\leq q\\leq s+1$, applying $S$ to the entries on any upper codimension-one face preserves setwise the projected cube coset $$\\pi\\left(h^{\\mathrm{diag}}C^q(H_\\bullet)\n                \\sigma^{\\mathrm{diag}}\\Lambda^{\\{0,1\\}^q}/\n                   \\Lambda^{\\{0,1\\}^q}\\right).\n \\label{eq:projected-face-coset}$$ Then there is a polynomial shear $T:\\mathfrak h\\to\\mathfrak h$ such that, for every $u\\in\\mathfrak h$, $$\\pi\\bigl(h\\exp(Tu)\\sigma\\Lambda\\bigr)\n =S\\bigl(\\pi(h\\exp(u)\\sigma\\Lambda)\\bigr).\n \\label{eq:joint-lift-identity}$$ The lift is a bijection of the cover. Its action on the other component is unrestricted. This conclusion applies separately to every point coset from Proposition <a href=\"#prop:local-cube-law\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:local-cube-law\">33</a>; no measurable choice of lifts as a function of $\\beta,r$ is required.",
      "explanation": "A symmetry of one marginal that is a face symmetry of projected cube cosets lifts to a polynomial shear of the joint cover.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **admissible family** ([source](#stmt-def-admissible)): For a fixed $n$, an admissible family consists of positive integers $M,h_1,\ldots,h_n,H_1,\ldots,H_n$, positive powers of two $X_1,\ldots,X_n$, and independent random variables $t_1,\ldots,t_n$, all depending on $w$, with the following properties.
- **system of piecewise models** ([source](#stmt-def-piecewise-model)): Fix finite sets $\mathcal A\subset\mathbb Q_{>0}$ and $[r]$, and an admissible family. A system of step-at-most-$s$ models consists of functions $S_{B,a,c}:\mathbb Z\to[0,1]$, one for each block $B$, $a\in\mathcal A$, and $c\in[r]$.
- **block** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A *block* is a set $B=T\cup\{i\}$ with $\varnothing\ne T<i$.
- **w-smooth integer** ([source](#pm-h-6)): An integer is *$w$-smooth* if all its prime factors are at most $w$.
- **dominates powers** ([source](#pm-h-6)): We say that a positive quantity $A$ *dominates powers* of a quantity $B\ge2$ if $A/B^C\to\infty$ for every fixed $C>0$.
- **bounded complexity** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Throughout this paper a family has *bounded complexity* if its nilmanifolds belong to a fixed finite list and its observables have uniformly bounded supremum and Lipschitz norms for fixed smooth metrics.

      "exact_definition": "We say that a positive quantity $A$ *dominates powers* of a quantity $B\\ge2$ if $A/B^C\\to\\infty$ for every fixed $C>0$.",
      "interpretation": "Scale separation used to make error terms negligible.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "dominates powers",
      "scope": "Growth conditions on cutoffs and gap lengths"
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "pm-h-7",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-7",
          "exact_quote": "Throughout this paper a family has *bounded complexity* if its nilmanifolds belong to a fixed finite list and its observables have uniformly bounded supremum and Lipschitz norms for fixed smooth metrics.",
          "source_id": "PM-164.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "Throughout this paper a family has *bounded complexity* if its nilmanifolds belong to a fixed finite list and its observables have uniformly bounded supremum and Lipschitz norms for fixed smooth metrics.",

|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-union-cardinality | number of distinct elements in the union of subset sums and products | = | $2(2^m-1)-m$ | set size m |
| q-calibration-tolerance | calibration tolerance | < | $1/4$ | tolerance tau |
| q-mask-count | number of multiplicative masks | = | $2^m-1$ | chain length m |
| q-row-template-bound | number of row templates after mask removal | ≤ | $q_{\mathrm{mask}}2^{q_{\mathrm{mask}}}$ | chain length m |
| q-correlation-exponent | exponent in the correlation test | = | $2^{-(q_{\mathrm{mask}}+d)}$ | depends only on m |
| q-dual-moment-constant | moment constant for dual tests | = | $2^{2^{d_*}-1}$ | dimension bound d_* |
| q-alignment-harmonic-mass | conditional harmonic alignment probability | ≥ | $1/(3Q_i)$ | fixed tau and model family |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-chain-selection** [claim](#stmt-lem-chain-selection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-chain-selection): Finite Ramsey by cardinality plus the finite sums theorem -> A chain whose block products have all nonempty products in one color
- **step-sampling** [claim](#stmt-lem-sampling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sampling): Periodic counting and partial summation for harmonic laws -> Total-mass bounds for residue, translation and dilation
- **step-product-law** [claim](#stmt-cor-product-law) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-product-law): Condition on the tail product and apply the dilation estimate -> Block product law close to weighted pivot law
- **step-master-scales** [claim](#stmt-lem-master-scales) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-master-scales): Sequential choice of scales using geometric exponents and prime pools -> Admissible scales and prime pools
- **step-rough-coprimality** [claim](#stmt-lem-rough-coprimality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rough-coprimality): Induction on number of variables with Brun–Titchmarsh bounds -> Coprime rough polynomial values with high probability
- **step-linear-forms** [claim](#stmt-prop-linear-forms) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-linear-forms): Local kernel counts, domination by an independent-valuation law, summation of excesses -> Mean-one estimate for divisor weights
- **step-mask-removal** [claim](#stmt-lem-mask-removal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mask-removal): Prime substitution and weighted Cauchy–Schwarz, one mask at a time -> Correlation over nonparallel row templates
- **step-row-directions** [claim](#stmt-lem-row-directions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-row-directions): Explicit kernel vectors and a deterministic parameter choice -> Integer translation directions with prescribed responses
- **step-additive-elimination** [claim](#stmt-lem-additive-elimination) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-additive-elimination): Weighted Cauchy–Schwarz along kernel directions and second-moment replacement of weights -> Cube average of the target function
- **step-correlation-test** [claim](#stmt-prop-correlation-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-correlation-test): Identify the law of the cube root with the pivot harmonic law -> One-variable cube estimate
- **step-dual-pseudorandomness** [claim](#stmt-lem-dual-pseudorandomness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-dual-pseudorandomness): Insert translation parameters and eliminate rows by weighted Cauchy–Schwarz -> Orthogonality and moment bounds for dual tests
- **step-dense-model** [claim](#stmt-prop-dense-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-dense-model): Minimax with polynomial approximation of the positive part -> Bounded dense models
- **step-energy-selection** [claim](#stmt-lem-energy-selection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-energy-selection): Finite Ramsey on binned projection energies -> Coarse models with controlled fine projections
- **step-cube-corner** [claim](#stmt-lem-cube-corner) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cube-corner): Face coordinates, compactness and Stone–Weierstrass -> Continuous missing-corner reconstruction
- **step-nilsequence-testing** [claim](#stmt-lem-nilsequence-testing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-nilsequence-testing): Realize reconstruction recipes as dual tests -> Dense model tests piecewise nilsequences
- **step-subgroup-inverse** [claim](#stmt-lem-subgroup-inverse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-subgroup-inverse): Subgroup norms, Bessel inequality and the inverse theorem in cyclic cells -> Small projection implies small cube average
- **step-prediction-completion** [claim](#stmt-pr-prediction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-subgroup-inverse-2): Order of parameter choices and telescoping replacement of color weights -> Calibration and counting comparison
- **step-rough-interpolation** [claim](#stmt-lem-rough-interpolation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rough-interpolation): Lagrange interpolation and exact polynomial division -> Integral rational polynomial with smooth denominators
- **step-rough-step** [claim](#stmt-prop-rough-step) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rough-step): Induction on dimension with Szemerédi, van der Waerden and kernel of a horizontal character -> Removal of a rough step
- **step-upper-face** [claim](#stmt-lem-upper-face-coordinates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-upper-face-coordinates): Direct sum of face Lie algebras and ideal filtration -> Unique face coordinates
- **step-adapted-factorization** [claim](#stmt-lem-adapted-factorization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-adapted-factorization): Taylor coordinates and finite filtration descent -> Smooth, equidistributed and periodic factorization
- **step-local-cube-law** [claim](#stmt-prop-local-cube-law) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-local-cube-law): Quantitative Leibman obstruction contradicting level irrationality -> Local Haar laws for point and cube averages
- **step-face-lift** [claim](#stmt-prop-face-lift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-face-lift): Projection to the marginal group, graded identity, polynomial shear on the cover -> Joint polynomial shear
- **step-shear-haar** [claim](#stmt-lem-shear-haar) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-shear-haar): Nilpotent operator algebra and total variation on expanding weighted boxes -> Nilpotent shear group and common Haar limit
- **step-finite-word-plan** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-word-plan) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-word-plan): Backward recursion using nilpotent polynomial recurrence and compactness of colorings -> Finite plan of scale updates and word jumps
- **step-conditional-face-invariance** [claim](#stmt-lem-conditional-face-invariance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-conditional-face-invariance): Staged exposure, removal of tail factors via rough step comparison, countable diagonal -> Marginal face invariance in probability
- **step-alignment-mass** [claim](#stmt-lem-alignment-mass) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-alignment-mass): Contradiction, lifted shears, Portmanteau on open and closed comparison sets -> Positive conditional alignment probability
- **step-alignment-completion** [claim](#stmt-pr-alignment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-alignment-mass-2): Iterate over pivots, translating slots back to raw variables -> Uniform lower bound on alignment probability
- **step-main-deduction** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-chain-selection-2): Combine chain selection, Prediction, Alignment and a union bound along the ultrafilter -> Monochromatic separated configuration
- **step-distinct-expressions** [claim](#stmt-cor-distinct-expressions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-chain-selection-3): Cancellation and comparison of greatest indices under the separation -> Injective subset sums and products meeting only at generators
- **step-finite-interval** [claim](#stmt-cor-finite-interval) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-finite-interval): Residue refinement and compactness over a finitely branching tree -> Finite interval form

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-main-monochromatic-sums-products**: For any finite coloring and any prescribed size, there exist distinct positive integers whose nonempty subset sums and subset products all share one color, with the elements growing fast enough that expressions do not collide.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-infinite-sequence**: Assuming the theorem yields an infinite sequence whose terms, pairwise sums and pairwise products all have one color. -> The result concerns prescribed finite sets only; a finite coloring without any such infinite set is known.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The proof of the main theorem is non-constructive: it combines a nonprincipal ultrafilter limit, compactness over infinite trees of colorings, and existence statements about nilsequence models. The source states that the compactness argument gives a finite bound but supplies no numerical estimate, so there is no finite procedure to implement and test.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main theorem is an existence result for finite sets under arbitrary finite colorings. Its proof goes through an ultrafilter limit, so it gives no program that finds the set. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper discusses no game, simulation or dynamics model. Colorings of the positive integers are purely combinatorial objects. |
| tools | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | The finite-interval corollary could serve as a specification for a checker or benchmark generator on bounded colorings. Because the interval bound is non-constructive, this stays at the level of a specification. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The theorem and its proof contain no finite procedure with explicit steps. The compactness step supplies no computable bound on the interval size. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No data structure appears in the paper. Subset sums and products are mathematical sets, not stored representations. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no computation to accelerate. Its results are existence and uniformity statements. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The pseudorandomness estimates concern weighted harmonic sampling on integers. They carry no security notion or hardness assumption. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The nilsequence models approximate color indicators inside a proof. No learning problem, training procedure or generalization claim exists. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective function or optimisation problem occurs. Tolerances are chosen only to make error terms small. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper says nothing about processes, messages or consensus. Its colorings are static. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result belongs to pure Ramsey-type combinatorics and higher-order Fourier analysis, which are not engineering domains. No further engineering use is grounded in the text. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-finite-sets-only** (hypothesis): The main theorem and its corollary concern prescribed finite sets of fixed size; they do not assert an infinite sequence with monochromatic sums and products. Blocked use: ['Deriving an infinite monochromatic sum-product sequence from the theorem', 'Applying the result to unbounded online streams']
- **limit-no-numerical-interval-bound** (nonconstructive): The finite interval form comes from a compactness argument over finitely branching trees of colorings and supplies no numerical estimate for the interval size. Blocked use: ['Allocating a search space of a concrete size N for finding the configuration', 'Quoting a running time or search bound']
- **limit-qualitative-parameters** (formal_scope): All parameters are qualitative: finiteness and order of choice matter, but no numerical bound for the smallest configuration is claimed. Blocked use: ['Using the paper to bound the least size of a monochromatic configuration', 'Reading the tolerance choices as an optimised scheme']
- **limit-integers-not-dilation-invariant** (edge_case): Passing from rationals to integers needs more than clearing denominators, since a dilation scales a sum and a product of several entries by different powers. Blocked use: ['Transferring the rational-case result to the integers by a common dilation', 'Extending the statement to real or continuous scalings']
- **limit-ultrafilter-extraction** (nonconstructive): The final deduction selects a parameter inside a set belonging to a fixed nonprincipal ultrafilter, so the configuration is obtained by an existence argument and not by an explicit procedure. Blocked use: ['Treating the proof as an algorithm that outputs the set', 'Assuming a fixed witnessing chain across different values of the parameter']
- **limit-alignment-no-uniform-rate** (approximation): The Alignment Principle gives a uniform positive lower bound for the success probability but no convergence rate uniform over model complexities. Blocked use: ['Quoting a rate of convergence independent of model complexity', 'Applying the alignment bound at finite parameter values without the limit']
- **limit-pure-sums-products** (formal_scope): The theorem concerns pure nonempty subset sums and pure nonempty subset products of one set; no mixed polynomial expression is covered. Blocked use: ['Claiming monochromaticity for mixed expressions such as a product plus another element']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove Hindman's finite sums and products conjecture: for every finite coloring of the positive integers and every positive integer <i>k</i>, there is a <i>k</i>-element set whose nonempty subset sums and nonempty subset products all have the same color.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Write $[n]=\{1,\ldots,n\}$ for a positive integer $n$. For a finite set $A\subset\mathbb N=\{1,2,\ldots\}$, write $$\mathop{\mathrm{FS}}(A)=\left\{\sum_{a\in B}a:\varnothing\ne B\subseteq A\right\},
 \qquad
 \mathop{\mathrm{FP}}(A)=\left\{\prod_{a\in B}a:\varnothing\ne B\subseteq A\right\}.$$ Thus each element of $A$ may occur at most once in an individual sum or product, and singleton subsets are included. We prove the following.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:separated-elements"></a>
**Theorem 1**. *Let $r,m\ge1$ be integers, and fix real numbers $R\ge2$ and $D\ge1$. For every coloring $\chi:\mathbb N\to[r]$, there are distinct positive integers $a_1<\cdots<a_m$ and a color $c\in[r]$ such that $$\chi\left(\sum_{j\in J}a_j\right)
 =\chi\left(\prod_{j\in J}a_j\right)=c
 \quad\text{for every }\varnothing\ne J\subseteq[m].$$ Equivalently, $\mathop{\mathrm{FS}}(A)\cup\mathop{\mathrm{FP}}(A)$ is monochromatic for a set $A\subset\mathbb N$ of cardinality $m$. The elements can additionally be chosen to satisfy $$\label{eq:separated-elements}
 a_1>R,\qquad
 a_d>R\left(\sum_{k<d}a_k+\prod_{k<d}a_k\right)^D
 \quad(2\le d\le m).$$*

</div>

Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> resolves Hindman’s finite sums and products conjecture positively. The same assertion for colorings of $\mathbb N_0=\{0,1,2,\ldots\}$ follows by restriction to $\mathbb N$. The separation in <a href="#eq:separated-elements" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:separated-elements">[eq:separated-elements]</a> also eliminates every collision between expressions except the shared singleton values.

<a id="stmt-cor-distinct-expressions"></a>
<div id="cor:distinct-expressions" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2**. *For every finite coloring of $\mathbb N$ and every $m\ge1$, there is an $m$-element set $A\subset\mathbb N$ for which $\mathop{\mathrm{FS}}(A)\cup\mathop{\mathrm{FP}}(A)$ is monochromatic and $$|\mathop{\mathrm{FS}}(A)|=|\mathop{\mathrm{FP}}(A)|=2^m-1,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{FS}}(A)\cap\mathop{\mathrm{FP}}(A)=A.$$ In particular, the union has $2(2^m-1)-m$ distinct elements.*

</div>

The proof of the corollary is elementary and appears at the end of Section <a href="#sec:framework" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:framework">2</a>. Both conclusions concern prescribed finite sets; the separation does not assert the existence of an infinite simultaneous sequence.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### History and significance

The separate additive and multiplicative problems have classical answers. Schur’s theorem guarantees a monochromatic triple $\{x,y,x+y\}$ in every finite coloring of $\mathbb N$ <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Schur](#ref-Schur)]. The finite sums theorem of Folkman–Rado–Sanders extends this to the nonempty subset sums of arbitrarily large finite sets; Sanders’s original argument appears in <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Sanders](#ref-Sanders), Theorem 2 and Corollary 1.1]. Hindman proved the infinite finite sums theorem <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Hindman](#ref-Hindman)]. Applying an additive theorem to the coloring $n\mapsto\chi(2^n)$ gives its multiplicative counterpart. The simultaneous question is different: Hindman constructed a finite coloring of $\mathbb N$ with no infinite set whose terms, pairwise sums and pairwise products all have one color <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Hindman1980](#ref-Hindman1980)]. His finite conjecture asks for arbitrarily large finite sets instead <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Hindman1979](#ref-Hindman1979)]; see also <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[HindmanStrauss](#ref-HindmanStrauss), Question 17.18].

For two variables, computer-assisted arguments of Graham and Hindman already established the existence of a monochromatic quartet $\{x,y,x+y,xy\}$ in every two-coloring of $\mathbb N$; see the historical account in <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[HindmanPhulara](#ref-HindmanPhulara), p. 82]. Bowen gave a proof in which $x$ and $y$ are distinct and exceed any prescribed bound, and more generally controlled the individual variables, their prefix products and their total sum <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[BowenTwoColors](#ref-BowenTwoColors), Theorem 1.1]. For arbitrary finite colorings, Moreira proved the existence of monochromatic triples $\{x,x+y,xy\}$ as part of a wider theorem on prefix products and polynomial shifts <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Moreira](#ref-Moreira), Theorem 1.4 and Corollary 1.5]. Alweiss gave a polynomial proof of the mixed triple and related polynomial-shift patterns <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[AlweissPolynomials](#ref-AlweissPolynomials), Theorem 1.2]. More recently, Alweiss, Bowen and Sabok proved that every two-coloring contains $\{x,y,xy,x+iy:1\le i\le k\}$ in one color for every prescribed $k$ <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[AlweissBowenSabok](#ref-AlweissBowenSabok), Theorem 1.2].

Results over fields developed in parallel. Green and Sanders proved the four-term result in sufficiently large prime fields <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[GreenSanders](#ref-GreenSanders), Theorem 1.2], while Bergelson and Moreira developed affine ergodic methods for mixed additive–multiplicative patterns <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[BergelsonMoreiraField](#ref-BergelsonMoreiraField), [BergelsonMoreiraAffine](#ref-BergelsonMoreiraAffine)]. Bowen and Sabok established $\{x,y,x+y,xy\}$ over $\mathbb Q$ <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[BowenSabok](#ref-BowenSabok), Theorem 1.1], and Alweiss proved the full finite sums and products theorem over $\mathbb Q$ <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Alweiss](#ref-Alweiss), Theorem 1.3]. Richter obtained integer density theorems for the mixed pair $\{x+Q(y),xy\}$ using Fourier analysis, ergodic theory and logarithmic averages over products of primes <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Richter](#ref-Richter), Theorems 1.5 and 1.7].

The passage from rational to integer configurations requires more than clearing denominators. A common dilation scales a sum by one power of the dilation and a product of $j$ entries by its $j$th power; an arbitrary coloring need not respect these different changes. The ordered-block selection in our proof follows the Ramsey and finite-sums argument in <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Alweiss](#ref-Alweiss), Section 5]. Our Alignment argument also adapts the compensating scale updates in <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Alweiss](#ref-Alweiss), Section 4], which fix the current target product while retaining the requirements secured at earlier targets. The additional task is to control the additive shifts arithmetically while preserving all the required multiplicative colors.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Proof strategy

The finite combinatorial part of the argument uses ordered blocks of widely separated integer variables. A Ramsey argument and the finite sums theorem select block products whose nonempty products already have one color. The remaining task is to ensure that their sums have that same color. We do this by constructing bounded predictions for the color indicators and then arranging that those predictions remain positive at the required sums.

The proof separates these tasks into a Prediction Principle and an Alignment Principle. Prediction replaces the color functions inside the relevant counts by piecewise nilsequences (Lipschitz functions evaluated along nilpotent orbits, separately on intervals and residue classes), while retaining the multiplicative color masks. It also ensures that a color occurring at a center is unlikely to have a small predicted value there. Alignment selects from a fixed finite list of rational scale vectors so that a positive prediction at every center remains positive after all the required additive shifts. Its success probability is bounded below independently of the complexity of the nilsequence models. This uniformity permits the final choice of small calibration errors.

Three constructions provide the analytic and arithmetic inputs.

1.  *Weights for the distribution of a block product.* Each chosen element is a product of several raw variables, so its distribution differs from that of a single variable at the same largest scale. We encode divisibility by the earlier variables in nonnegative weights and attach the same weight to every sum ending at that block. Prime substitutions and weighted Cauchy–Schwarz remove the product-color conditions and reduce the counting error to one-variable additive tests.

2.  *Predictions at two additive scales.* The counting estimate tests short additive shifts, whereas Alignment needs one model valid across a larger interval. We construct nilsequence models on nested intervals and use Ramsey selection to make their projections close. This supplies a common model for both requirements, with nilpotence step depending only on the requested configuration size.

3.  *Realizing shifts through nilsequence states.* Adding an earlier block product may require a noninteger change in the largest-scale variable of that block. We first make an integer residue correction and then realize the remaining displacement as a transformation of the nilsequence model. Two separate arguments justify this transformation: comparison of averages on arithmetic progressions, and a lifting theorem that makes transformations for different models act on one common state. These transformations generate a nilpotent group whose step is independent of model complexity. Finite polynomial recurrence in this group gives the required uniform alignment probability.

The rough-step comparison and the passage from marginal cube symmetries to joint lifts are stated separately in Propositions <a href="#prop:rough-step" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:rough-step">28</a> and <a href="#prop:face-lift" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:face-lift">34</a>. Their hypotheses make explicit the joint polynomial dependence and the cube information needed to control otherwise unavailable shifts. The proof uses the inverse theorem of Green, Tao and Ziegler <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[GTZ](#ref-GTZ), [GTZErratum](#ref-GTZErratum)], the concatenation theorem of Tao and Ziegler <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[TaoZiegler](#ref-TaoZiegler)], quantitative polynomial equidistribution <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[GreenTao](#ref-GreenTao), [GreenTaoErratum](#ref-GreenTaoErratum)], and nilpotent polynomial recurrence <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[ZorinKranich](#ref-ZorinKranich)]. We state the required forms when they are used. The intervening transference, progression-comparison and lifting arguments are proved below.

Section <a href="#sec:framework" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:framework">2</a> gives the two principles and derives Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> from them, including integrality and separation. Sections <a href="#sec:arithmetic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:arithmetic">3</a>–<a href="#sec:prediction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:prediction">5</a> develop Prediction; Sections <a href="#sec:rough-progressions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:rough-progressions">6</a>–<a href="#sec:alignment" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:alignment">8</a> develop Alignment. The missing-corner Lemma <a href="#lem:cube-corner" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:cube-corner">31</a>, proved independently in Section <a href="#sec:cube-limits" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cube-limits">7</a>, is also used in Prediction. All parameters are qualitative: their finiteness and order of choice are essential, but no numerical bound for the smallest configuration is claimed.

<figure id="fig:proof-map">

<figcaption>The main analytic routes meet in the combinatorial deduction. The diagonal arrow records the shared missing-corner reconstruction from Lemma <a href="#lem:cube-corner" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:cube-corner">31</a>. Alignment fixes a positive mass before Prediction fixes model complexity; this order permits the counting error to be made small enough.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="sec:framework"></a>
### Two principles and the combinatorial deduction

We first state the two analytic principles in the precise forms used to prove Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. This also fixes the order of all parameters. The proof of the Prediction Principle occupies Sections <a href="#sec:arithmetic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:arithmetic">3</a>–<a href="#sec:prediction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:prediction">5</a>, with the independent missing-corner Lemma <a href="#lem:cube-corner" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:cube-corner">31</a> proved in Section <a href="#sec:cube-limits" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cube-limits">7</a>. Sections <a href="#sec:rough-progressions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:rough-progressions">6</a> and <a href="#sec:cube-limits" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cube-limits">7</a> develop the nilsequence tools for the Alignment Principle, proved in Section <a href="#sec:alignment" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:alignment">8</a>.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Ordered blocks and admissible scales

For nonempty subsets $A,B\subseteq[n]$, $A<B$ means $\max A<\min B$; we write $A<i$ for $A<\{i\}$. For a list of multiplicative quantities $u_1,\ldots,u_n$, put $u_A=\prod_{j\in A}u_j$. In particular this convention applies to $t_A,h_A,b_A$, and $x_A$. Empty products, when they occur, equal one.

<a id="eq:adding-blocks"></a>
<a id="eq:block-chain"></a>
A *block* is a set $B=T\cup\{i\}$ with $\varnothing\ne T<i$. We call $i$ its *pivot* and $T$ its *tail*. For such a block let $$\label{eq:adding-blocks}
 \mathcal E_B=
 \bigl\{P\cup\{j\}:\varnothing\ne P<T<j<i\bigr\}.$$ These are the blocks whose values will be added at the anchor $B$. A *chain of length $m$* is a list $B_d=T_d\cup\{i_d\}$, $d\in[m]$, satisfying $$\label{eq:block-chain}
 \varnothing\ne T_1<T_2<\cdots<T_m<i_1<\cdots<i_m.$$ All blocks in a chain are disjoint. Moreover, if $k<d$, then $B_k\in\mathcal E_{B_d}$. This last property is why we use this particular ordering of tails and pivots.

All asymptotic parameters below are indexed by positive integers $w\to\infty$, and $$W=W(w)=\prod_{p\le w}p,$$ where the product is over primes. An integer is *$w$-smooth* if all its prime factors are at most $w$. We say that a positive quantity $A$ *dominates powers* of a quantity $B\ge2$ if $A/B^C\to\infty$ for every fixed $C>0$. Every finite index set, complexity bound and tolerance is held fixed in this convention unless additional uniformity is stated. Sampling an interval always means sampling its integer points.

Fix once and for all a nonprincipal ultrafilter $\mathcal U$ on the positive integers $w$, and write $\lim_{\mathcal U}$ for its limit. Every bounded real sequence has such a limit, and it agrees with the ordinary limit when that exists. Every set belonging to $\mathcal U$ meets every sufficiently late range of $w$. These are the limit and extraction properties used below.

<a id="stmt-def-admissible"></a>
<div id="def:admissible" class="definition">

**Definition 3** (Admissible parameters). For a fixed $n$, an admissible family consists of positive integers $M,h_1,\ldots,h_n,H_1,\ldots,H_n$, positive powers of two $X_1,\ldots,X_n$, and independent random variables $t_1,\ldots,t_n$, all depending on $w$, with the following properties.

1.  $M$ and each $h_i$ are $w$-smooth multiples of $W^w$. For every block $B$, and also for every singleton $\{i\}$, $h_B\le M$. For every $A\in\mathcal E_B$, the ratio $h_A/h_B$ is an integer multiple of $W^w$.

2.  Each $H_i$ is a multiple of $M$ and dominates powers of $2+M+\prod_{\ell<i}X_\ell$. The quantity $\log X_i$ dominates powers of $H_i$.

3.  The law of $t_i$ is the harmonic $W$-unit probability measure <a id="eq:harmonic-law"></a>$$\label{eq:harmonic-law}
     \mu_i(y)=\frac{\mathbf 1_{X_i\le y<X_i^2}\mathbf 1_{(y,W)=1}}{Z_i y},
     \qquad
     Z_i=\sum_{\substack{X_i\le y<X_i^2\\(y,W)=1}}\frac1y.$$

</div>

Fixed positive rational factors have only finitely many denominator primes and fixed denominator valuations. Consequently they can be absorbed by $W^w$ for all sufficiently large $w$. In particular, every color argument used below is eventually a positive integer. For a chain and fixed positive rational $a_1,\ldots,a_m$, the ratios $h_{B_k}a_k/(h_{B_d}a_d)$, $k<d$, are eventually positive integers: the pair belongs to <a href="#eq:adding-blocks" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:adding-blocks">[eq:adding-blocks]</a>. These facts allow us to use rational scale lists without ever evaluating the coloring on a noninteger.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Piecewise nilsequence models

A *nilsequence of step at most $s$* is a sequence <a id="eq:nilsequence-definition"></a>$$\label{eq:nilsequence-definition}
 k\longmapsto F(g^k x),\qquad k\in\mathbb Z,$$ where $G$ is a connected simply connected nilpotent Lie group of step at most $s$, $\Gamma\subset G$ is a lattice, $x\in G/\Gamma$, $g\in G$, and $F$ is a Lipschitz function on $G/\Gamma$. Throughout this paper a family has *bounded complexity* if its nilmanifolds belong to a fixed finite list and its observables have uniformly bounded supremum and Lipschitz norms for fixed smooth metrics. No bound is imposed on the translating elements $g$ or the points $x$. Constants are included. Products and Lipschitz combinations of any fixed finite number of such families remain bounded-complexity nilsequences of the same maximal step, by taking product nilmanifolds.

<a id="stmt-def-piecewise-model"></a>
<div id="def:piecewise-model" class="definition">

**Definition 4** (Piecewise models). Fix finite sets $\mathcal A\subset\mathbb Q_{>0}$ and $[r]$, and an admissible family. A system of step-at-most-$s$ models consists of functions $S_{B,a,c}:\mathbb Z\to[0,1]$, one for each block $B$, $a\in\mathcal A$, and $c\in[r]$. If $i=\max B$, then on each interval $[kH_i,(k+1)H_i)$ and each residue class modulo $M$, the function is a nilsequence in the progression index. The complexity is bounded uniformly over $w$, all pieces, and all model indices. The representing observables may be, and are, chosen $[0,1]$-valued on their entire nilmanifolds.

</div>

The sequence and its nilmanifold may change from piece to piece within the fixed finite list. This freedom is necessary in the inverse-theorem argument. Real parts followed by clipping to $[0,1]$ justify the final sentence of Definition <a href="#def:piecewise-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:piecewise-model">4</a> without increasing the nilpotence step.

For a block $B=T\cup\{i\}$ define its divisor weight on all of $\mathbb Z$ by <a id="eq:divisor-weight"></a>$$\label{eq:divisor-weight}
 \nu_B(y)=\mathbb E_{\sigma=t_T}\sigma\mathbf 1_{\sigma\mid y}.$$ The expectation uses the raw laws of the tail variables only. For a fixed tail product $\sigma$, we have $(\sigma,W)=1$, so the conditional mass of $\sigma t_i$ at $y$ is $$\frac{\sigma\mathbf 1_{\sigma\mid y}\mathbf 1_{(y,W)=1}}{Z_i y}
 \quad\text{on }[\sigma X_i,\sigma X_i^2).$$ Thus $\nu_B$ averages the divisibility factor inserted by multiplication by the tail. Corollary <a href="#cor:product-law" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:product-law">10</a> controls the change of endpoints back to $[X_i,X_i^2)$ and shows that the sum of the absolute differences between the masses of $\operatorname{Law}(t_B)$ and $\nu_B\mu_i$ tends to zero. Whenever several copies of a weight are expanded, they receive independent divisor samples. In particular, two occurrences of $\nu_B$ do not share a sample unless this is expressly stated.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Prediction and alignment

<a id="eq:scale-list-closure"></a>
We use finite lists $\mathcal B\subset\mathbb Q_{>0}^n$ and $\mathcal A\subset\mathbb Q_{>0}$ satisfying $$\label{eq:scale-list-closure}
 b_B\in\mathcal A\quad\text{for all }b\in\mathcal B
 \text{ and all blocks }B.$$ The coloring is a map $\chi:\mathbb N\to[r]$.

<a id="stmt-pr-prediction"></a>
<div id="pr:prediction" class="principle">

**Principle 5** (Prediction). *For every $m\ge2$ there is an integer $s=s(m)$ with the following property. Given $n,r,\chi$, finite lists satisfying <a href="#eq:scale-list-closure" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scale-list-closure">[eq:scale-list-closure]</a>, and tolerances $0<\tau<1/4$, $\eta>0$, there are admissible parameters and step-at-most-$s$ models such that the following conclusions hold along the fixed ultrafilter $\mathcal U$ in $w$.*

<a id="eq:prediction-calibration"></a>
1.  *For each $B,a,c$, $$\label{eq:prediction-calibration}
     \lim_{\mathcal U}\mathbb P\bigl(
     \chi(h_Bat_B)=c,\ S_{B,a,c}(t_B)\le2\tau\bigr)
     \le3\tau+\eta.$$*

<a id="eq:weighted-count"></a>
<a id="eq:prediction-counting"></a>
2.  *Fix a chain $B_1,\ldots,B_m$, $b\in\mathcal B$ and a color $c\in[r]$. Put $$c_d=h_{B_d}b_{B_d},\qquad
     f_d(y)=\mathbf 1_{\chi(c_dy)=c},\qquad
     \nu_d=\nu_{B_d},\qquad S_d=S_{B_d,b_{B_d},c}.$$ Here $f_d$ is defined on positive integers and may be extended by zero elsewhere. For $\varnothing\ne J\subseteq[m]$ write $d(J)=\max J$ and <a id="eq:sum-forms"></a><a id="eq:product-mask"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
     L_J(z)&=\sum_{k\in J}\frac{c_k}{c_{d(J)}}z_k,
     \label{eq:sum-forms}\\
     U(z)&=\prod_{\varnothing\ne J\subseteq[m]}
     \mathbf 1_{\chi(\prod_{k\in J}c_kz_k)=c}.
     \label{eq:product-mask}
    \end{aligned}$$ Then the nonnegative weighted count $$\label{eq:weighted-count}
     \mathcal I_{b,c,\mathbf B}=
     \mathbb E_{z\sim\bigotimes_{d=1}^m\mu_{i_d}}
     U(z)\prod_{d=1}^m\nu_d(z_d)
     \prod_{\substack{J\subseteq[m]\\|J|\ge2}}
     (f_{d(J)}\nu_{d(J)})(L_J(z))$$ satisfies, for $z(t)_d=t_{B_d}$, $$\label{eq:prediction-counting}
     \lim_{\mathcal U}
     \abs*{\mathcal I_{b,c,\mathbf B}
     -\mathbb E_t U(z(t))
     \prod_{\substack{J\subseteq[m]\\|J|\ge2}}
     S_{d(J)}(L_J(z(t)))}\le\eta.$$*

</div>

The step bound depends only on the requested chain length $m$. The complexity bound may depend on all the fixed data and tolerances. The extra weights on the sum forms in <a href="#eq:weighted-count" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weighted-count">[eq:weighted-count]</a> are deliberate: they put the function at each sum under the same majorant as the function at its last summand. Positivity of this weighted count still gives an actual monochromatic configuration.

<a id="stmt-pr-alignment"></a>
<div id="pr:alignment" class="principle">

<a id="eq:alignment-implication"></a>
<a id="eq:alignment-probability"></a>
**Principle 6** (Alignment). *For every $n,r,s$ there are finite lists $\mathcal B,\mathcal A$ satisfying <a href="#eq:scale-list-closure" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scale-list-closure">[eq:scale-list-closure]</a> and a constant $\delta>0$, depending only on $n,r,s$, such that the following holds.
